# Verb reference

Do not edit this file by hand. A test writes it from the verb registry, the same table that `tuios list-verbs` prints.
To write it again, run `go test ./internal/session -run TestProtocolVerbsDocMatchesRegistry -update`.

[protocol.md](protocol.md) tells how to call a verb and what the errors mean.
It also has more about the verbs that need it.

### `add-reply-history`

Keep a message the person sent to an agent in the reply history. The same message again moves to the end. A message keeps at most 256 KiB of pastes and images: past that the largest are dropped and named. Only the person adds to it, with a live human_nonce.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `target` | string | yes |  | The key the client keeps the history under. |
| `parts` | []object | yes |  | The message, as queue-prompt takes it. |
| `human_nonce` | string |  |  | The nonce from the attach reply of a client attached now. The TUI sends its own. Without a live one the call is not the person's. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `dropped` | []string | The blocks too large to keep. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"add-reply-history","params":{"target":"claude-code:/work/api","parts":[{"kind":"text","text":"rerun with -count=1"}],"human_nonce":"<from the attach reply>"}}
```

### `add-task`

Add a task card to a session: one unit of work, with a title, the prompt for the pane that takes it, and a stage. The card is kept by the daemon and saved with the session, so it survives the pane that added it and a restart. Who added it (by) comes from the connection.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session` | string |  |  | Session name. Omit from a pane for the pane's own session, and from outside every pane for the most recently active one. |
| `title` | string | yes |  | One line that says what the card is, at most 200 bytes. Control characters are dropped. |
| `prompt` | string |  |  | What the pane that takes the card is to do, at most 16 KiB. |
| `stage` | string |  | `todo` | The stage it starts in. One of `todo`, `plan`, `doing`, `verify`, `review`, `shipped` or `done`. |
| `project` | string |  |  | The repository the card is for, by its origin URL. It then shows on that repository's board in every session and on linked machines, and a pane on another machine can claim it with host. Omit for a card of the session only. |
| `harness` | string |  |  | The agent the card is for, such as claude-code. Pull dispatch gives it only to that agent. Omit for any agent. |
| `ask_first` | bool |  | `false` | The card waits for a person to start it: dispatch never gives it to an agent. |
| `machine` | string |  |  | Where the card's agent starts: any for the machine place-agent picks, or a linked machine's name. Omit for this machine. |
| `human_nonce` | string |  |  | The attached client's nonce, to act as the person (by human). One that does not verify is refused with not_human. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | string | The card. |
| `session` | string | Its session. |
| `title` | string | Its title. |
| `preview` | string | The first line of its prompt, at most 80 characters. |
| `stage` | string | Its stage. One of `todo`, `plan`, `doing`, `verify`, `review`, `shipped` or `done`. |
| `stage_at` | int | When it entered the stage, Unix nanoseconds. |
| `holder` | object | The pane that holds it: window, name, harness, state (read from the pane now) and held_at. Null when no pane holds it. |
| `by` | string | Who added it: human, shell, the adding pane's window id, link:HOST or plugin:ID. |
| `at` | int | When it was added, Unix nanoseconds. |
| `blocked` | object | reason, by and at when the card is blocked, else null. |
| `fan` | object | fan and fan_id, for a card added in a fan's session. |
| `attachment_count` | int | How many attachments the card has. Absent for none. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"add-task","params":{"session":"work","title":"make the backoff jitter configurable","prompt":"Read api/retry.go and add a jitter option, with a test."}}
```

### `agent-activity`

Read what the agent in a pane has been doing, from the ring of hook events the daemon keeps for it: prompts, tool calls and their results, finished turns, subagents starting and stopping, conversations starting, and the commands its shell ran, the newest 256 of them. A pane gets a ring on the first hook report that carries activity, and loses it when it closes; the ring is daemon memory only. With recap, a summary of it since a time. The text is the agent's, cleaned and masked, and marked untrusted.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session` | string |  |  | Session name. Omit from a pane for the pane's own session, and from outside every pane for the most recently active one. |
| `window` | string |  |  | Window id or name. Omit to target the focused window. |
| `since` | int |  |  | Only entries after this time, in unix nanoseconds. |
| `since_seq` | int |  |  | Only entries after this seq. |
| `limit` | int |  | `64` | At most this many entries, newest last, up to 256. |
| `recap` | bool |  | `false` | Also summarise the entries: turns, files, commands, the last test run and what the agent last said. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `entries` | []object | Oldest first: seq, at (unix nanoseconds), kind (prompt, tool, tool_done, tool_failed, turn_end, subagent_start, subagent_stop, session_start, command or state), tool, target, files, ok, exit and text per entry. Empty for a pane with no ring. |
| `window` | string | The window id the entries are of. |
| `last_seq` | int | The seq of the newest entry the ring holds. |
| `recap` | object | With recap, over every entry after since and since_seq whatever the limit: since (where it starts), turns, files (the first 20), files_total, commands, tests (the newest test run: cmdline, ok or null when unknown, at), last_said and state (the pane's state now). |
| `untrusted` | bool | Always true: the text is the agent's. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"agent-activity","params":{"session":"work","window":"build","recap":true}}
```

### `agent-hook-event`

Run one harness hook event in the daemon, as tuios agent-hook runs it in a process of its own: the payload is read, the event mapped to the state it reports, and the report made for the pane the caller runs in. The pane is found from the process that sent the call (its TUIOS_PANE_ID, or its terminal session and ancestors), never from a param. The result holds what the hook would print for the harness to read. A call that waits on the Inbox for a permission prompt answers when the person does, or when the hold ends.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `harness` | string | yes |  | The harness whose hook fired, by id or alias, as tuios agent-hook takes it: claude-code, codex, gemini-cli and so on. |
| `event` | string |  |  | The event, for a harness whose payload does not name it. Omit it when the payload does. |
| `payload` | string |  |  | The hook's JSON payload as the harness wrote it, as a string. Omit it for an event with none. |
| `payload_json` | object |  |  | The payload as JSON rather than a string, for a caller that cannot quote it. Pass payload or payload_json, not both. |
| `env` | object |  |  | The variables of the hook process the mapping reads, by name. Only these are taken: TUIOS_AGENT, CODEX_THREAD_ID, CURSOR_VERSION, GROK_SESSION_ID, CRUSH_SESSION_ID. The pane's own variables are always read from the calling process. |
| `explain` | bool |  |  | Also return what was decided and why, as tuios agent-hook --explain prints it. |
| `forwarder` | bool |  |  | The call comes from the shell line tuios integration installs, which closes its sending half once the call is sent and cannot read JSON. The reply is then the line TUIOS-HOOK-OK and the hook's stdout as it is, with no JSON response, and the connection ends. A hold for the Inbox ends when the caller's process does, not at the end of its input. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `stdout` | string | What the hook prints for the harness to read: an empty object for a harness that parses its hook's output as JSON, the lines a session start adds to the agent's context, the line about unread mail, or a permission decision. Empty for most events. |
| `explain` | object or null | With explain: the decision, the pane it went to and how it was found, what the daemon answered, and any error. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"agent-hook-event","params":{"harness":"claude-code","payload":"{\"hook_event_name\":\"UserPromptSubmit\",\"session_id\":\"s1\",\"prompt\":\"fix the build\"}"}}
```

### `agent-mail-notice`

Answer with one line that tells a pane's agent about its unread mail, once per message. tuios agent-hook calls it for a harness that adds a hook's output to the agent's context. The line holds a count, the senders' names cut to letters, digits and . _ - : @, and the read-agent-messages command to run. It never holds a subject or a body. A message is announced once: the call marks the unread mail it describes as announced, and the next call answers with an empty notice until newer mail arrives.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session` | string |  |  | Session name. Omit from a pane for the pane's own session, and from outside every pane for the most recently active one. |
| `window` | string |  |  | Window id or name. Omit to target the focused window. |
| `reset` | bool |  | `false` | Announce all unread mail again, also mail announced before. A hook sets it when a conversation starts, resumes or is compacted, since the agent no longer holds the earlier notice. |
| `lease` | bool |  | `false` | Mark the mail announced for 3 seconds only, and answer with a lease token. The mail is announced for good when the caller sends the token back in commit. A hook that missed its deadline sends nothing back, and the next call announces the mail again. |
| `commit` | int |  |  | A lease token from an earlier call. The call then only makes that lease's mail announced for good, and answers with committed. |
| `harness` | string |  |  | Id of the harness whose hook asks. With it the call is held to the guard set-agent-state holds a hook's report to: a pane attributed to another harness, another conversation, or a harness outside the pane gets no notice, and the reason. |
| `agent_session_id` | string |  |  | The harness's id for the conversation, read as report-agent-activity reads it. |
| `harness_pid` | int |  |  | The pid of the harness process that ran the hook, read as report-agent-activity reads it. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `window_id` | string | The pane whose mail it is. |
| `unread` | int | How many messages in the pane's inbox are unread, seen ones included. |
| `notice` | string | The line to show the agent, empty when there is no unread mail or all of it was announced before. |
| `lease` | int | The token to send back in commit, for a call with lease that answered with a notice. |
| `committed` | bool | For a call with commit: whether the lease was still open and is now announced for good. |
| `reason` | string | Why the guard gave no notice. Absent when it did not refuse. One of `foreign_session`, `foreign_harness` or `foreign_pane`. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"agent-mail-notice","params":{"session":"work","window":"build"}}
```

### `agent-message-receipts`

The mail one window sent and its state, newest last: delivered, seen (the recipient looked at its inbox without reading) or read, with the times, and undeliverable when the recipient window is gone. Marks nothing. On this machine it reads every session's ring and asks each linked machine for the mail this window sent there; over a link it answers only for mail that arrived on that link from that window, with ids, recipients and times. A message a ring no longer holds is not listed.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `window` | string | yes |  | The sending window's id, normally $TUIOS_PANE_ID. A restricted connection may name only its own window. |
| `limit` | int |  | `20` | At most this many rows, newest last, at most 200. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `receipts` | []object | One per message: id, host (empty for this machine), session, to, to_label, thread_id, sent_at, seen_at, read_at (Unix nanoseconds, 0 when not yet), undeliverable, and on this machine preview, the first line of the text. |
| `waiting` | []object | Mail this window sent to a machine whose link is down, kept here until it is back: queue_id, host, session, to, queued_at. |
| `unreachable` | []object | Linked machines that could not be asked now: host and reason. Mail sent there is not listed. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"agent-message-receipts","params":{"window":"3f2a91c0-5b1e-4d7a-9c2f-0e8b6a4d1c3e"}}
```

### `agent-preset`

Merge an [agents.presets] entry of this daemon's config into start-agent params, as start-agent merges it, for a start-agent call on this machine or another one, so a preset of this machine works on every host. An unknown name is refused with the names there are.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `preset` | string |  |  | The preset to merge. |
| `params` | object |  |  | The start-agent params to merge it into. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `params` | object | The merged start-agent params, without preset. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"agent-preset","params":{"preset":"worker","params":{"name":"w3","session":"crew"}}}
```

### `agent-readiness`

Say for each harness whether it can start an agent on this machine: its program on the PATH, and for a harness whose manifest names a check ([launch] signed_in_args), whether it says it is signed in. The check runs the harness's own program, is bounded to 2 seconds, and its answer is kept for a minute; its output is matched and dropped.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `harnesses` | []string |  |  | Harness ids to answer for. Omit for every harness the registry knows. |
| `env` | object |  |  | PATH in it is where the programs are looked up, as for start-agent. Refused from another machine. |
| `repo_url` | string |  |  | Also say if this machine has a checkout of it. |
| `repos_root` | string |  |  | Where to look, as for start-agent. |
| `peer_instance` | string |  |  | The caller's daemon instance, for back. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `agents` | []object | One per harness, by id: harness, name, command, installed, signed_in (yes, no or unknown; absent for a harness with no check), usage_limit. |
| `repo` | string | found, none or many. |
| `load` | object | cores, load1, load_ok, mem_pct, working, starting, started, instance. |
| `cap` | int | [tasks] machine_cap, 0 for none. |
| `back` | string | This machine's name for the caller, when that link is up. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"agent-readiness","params":{}}
```

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"agent-readiness","params":{"harnesses":["claude-code","codex"]}}
```

### `agent-statusline-event`

Run one status line payload in the daemon, as tuios agent-statusline runs it without --then: the model, context use and cost it names go to the pane the caller runs in, as agent metadata, at most once every 15 seconds while they change. The pane is found from the process that sent the call, never from a param. It prints nothing; a caller with a status line of its own runs that itself.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `harness` | string | yes |  | The harness whose status line ran, such as claude-code. |
| `payload` | string |  |  | The status line payload as the harness wrote it, as a string. |
| `payload_json` | object |  |  | The payload as JSON rather than a string. Pass payload or payload_json, not both. |
| `env` | object |  |  | The variables of the calling process the mapping reads, by name. Only these are taken: TUIOS_AGENT, CODEX_THREAD_ID, CURSOR_VERSION, GROK_SESSION_ID, CRUSH_SESSION_ID. |
| `turn_end` | bool |  |  | The harness's turn just ended: changed values go now, whatever the interval, as tuios agent-statusline --turn-end. |
| `explain` | bool |  |  | Also return what was decided and why. |
| `forwarder` | bool |  |  | The call comes from the shell line tuios integration installs. The reply is then the line TUIOS-HOOK-OK, with no JSON response, and the connection ends. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `sent` | bool | Whether the values went to the pane. |
| `skip` | string | Why nothing was sent, such as unchanged values or the interval. Empty when sent. |
| `error` | string | What went wrong finding the pane or writing to it. Absent when nothing did. |
| `explain` | object or null | With explain: the values read, the pane and how it was found. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"agent-statusline-event","params":{"harness":"claude-code","payload_json":{"model":{"display_name":"Opus"},"context_window":{"used_percentage":42}}}}
```

### `agent-transcript`

Read the conversation of the agent in a pane, for a client that shows it to the person: prompts, answers, thinking, tool calls with their status, diffs of edits, plans and todo lists, decoded from the transcript the pane is joined to. Readers include Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI, Qwen Code, Copilot CLI, Amp, OpenCode, Cursor Agent and Pi. Only the person can read it, with a live human_nonce, as reply-approval checks it. Every string is the agent's or its tools', with control characters removed and likely secrets masked, so the reply is marked untrusted.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session` | string |  |  | Session name. Omit from a pane for the pane's own session, and from outside every pane for the most recently active one. |
| `window` | string | yes |  | The pane: a window id or name. |
| `human_nonce` | string | yes |  | The nonce of a client attached now, or of attach-presence on this connection. Without a live one the call is refused with not_human. A presence made for one session reads only that session's panes. |
| `after` | string |  |  | The cursor of an earlier reply. The reply then holds the entries after it. Omit it to read the newest limit entries. |
| `before` | string |  |  | The older cursor of an earlier reply. The reply then holds the newest limit entries before it. Do not give it with after. |
| `limit` | int |  | `200` | The most entries to return, 1 to 1000. Leave it out for the default. 0 is refused. |
| `image` | string |  |  | The id of an entry that has an image. The reply then holds that image's media_type and data (base64) in place of a page. after, before and limit are ignored. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `session` | string | The pane's session. |
| `window` | string | The pane's window id. |
| `harness` | string | The harness whose transcript was read. |
| `cursor` | string | Send it as after to read what comes next. It is opaque. |
| `reset` | bool | True when after was not a cursor into the file as it is now. The entries are then the newest limit, and the client starts its list again. |
| `more` | bool | True when a read after a cursor stopped before the end. Read again with the new cursor. |
| `older` | string | Send it as before to read the entries before this page. Empty when the page starts at the first entry. |
| `entries` | []object | id, at (unix ms), role (user, assistant, tool), kind (text, thinking, tool_call, tool_result, plan, todos), text, truncated, tool, target, status (ok, error, running), tool_id, diff, plan, todos and image. An image's entry has the text [image] and an image object with its media_type. Read its data with image set to the entry's id. Each diff line carries spans (syntax tokens with chroma CSS classes) and words (the changed part), in UTF-16 offsets. A diff with whole_replace true was not matched line by line: each hunk shows the changed old lines removed, then the changed new lines added. An entry with an id the client holds replaces it. |
| `media_type` | string | With image: image/png, image/jpeg, image/gif or image/webp. |
| `data` | string | With image: the image, base64, at most 10 MiB decoded. |
| `untrusted` | bool | Always true: the text is the agent's. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"agent-transcript","params":{"session":"work","window":"api","human_nonce":"<from attach-presence>"}}
```

```json
{"id":2,"verb":"agent-transcript","params":{"session":"work","window":"api","human_nonce":"<from attach-presence>","after":"<the cursor of the last reply>"}}
```

```json
{"id":3,"verb":"agent-transcript","params":{"session":"work","window":"api","human_nonce":"<from attach-presence>","before":"<the older cursor of the first reply>"}}
```

```json
{"id":4,"verb":"agent-transcript","params":{"session":"work","window":"api","human_nonce":"<from attach-presence>","image":"<an entry id>"}}
```

### `answer-ask`

Answer an ask-human question for the person. Only a client attached right now can, with the nonce from its attach reply: an agent never can. The answer must be one of the question's options. The first answer wins; a later one is answered with the one that stands and applied false.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `request_id` | string |  |  | The request_id of the ask item. |
| `answer` | string |  |  | One of the item's options. |
| `human_nonce` | string |  |  | The nonce the daemon issued in an attach reply, for a client attached now over the same kind of connection. The TUI sends its own. |
| `question` | string |  |  | The question the answer was picked from. When given and it is not the item's, nothing is answered: applied is false with reason changed. The TUI always sends it. |
| `upgrade_dialog` | bool |  |  | The answer is a key pressed in a client's live upgrade dialog. A live upgrade's ask takes only an answer that carries it. The TUI sends it from that dialog alone, and gui-bridge drops it. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `request_id` | string | The question answered. |
| `answer` | string | The answer that stands: this one when applied, an earlier one when not. |
| `applied` | bool | False when an earlier answer stands or the question changed. |
| `reason` | string | answered, or how the question ended before this answer, or changed. |
| `answered_by` | string | The id of the client whose answer stands. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"answer-ask","params":{"request_id":"9f86d081884c7d65","answer":"yes","human_nonce":"<from the attach reply>"}}
```

### `answer-prompt`

Answer the open prompt for the person. Only a client attached right now can, with the nonce from its attach reply: an agent, a plugin or a script never can. The first answer wins.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `request_id` | string |  |  | The id of the prompt the client shows. |
| `human_nonce` | string |  |  | The nonce the daemon issued in an attach reply, for a client attached now. The TUI sends its own. |
| `cancel` | bool |  |  | The person cancelled the prompt. It takes no value, ids or other. |
| `value` | string |  |  | For input: the text, one line, at most 4096 bytes. |
| `ids` | []string |  |  | For pick: the ids of the rows chosen. One, unless the prompt takes multiple. |
| `other` | string |  |  | For pick with allow_other: typed text that is not a row. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `request_id` | string | The prompt answered. |
| `applied` | bool | True when this answer ended the prompt. |
| `status` | string | answered or cancelled. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"answer-prompt","params":{"request_id":"9f86d081884c7d65","value":"main","human_nonce":"<from the attach reply>"}}
```

### `apply-config`

Apply config.toml now, including the changes that give panes or linked machines more: [agents.permissions], [hosts] and their link policies. A change to the file applies only what narrows those; the rest waits for this verb or a daemon restart. Only the person may call it: it is refused from inside a pane and over a link.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `host` | string |  |  | Apply only this host's entry, or its removal, and nothing else in the file. tuios hosts add sends it. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `mode` | string | [agents.permissions] mode now in force. |
| `default_grants` | []string | What a pane started with no grants of its own holds now. |
| `changes` | []string | What changed, one sentence each: the default grants, hosts added, removed or dialled another way, and link policies. |
| `still_waiting` | bool | True when config.toml still holds a change that waits, which happens when only one host was applied. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"apply-config"}
```

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"apply-config","params":{"host":"build"}}
```

### `artifact-list`

List what one pane has shown the person, newest first: the TML surfaces it opened, open or closed, and the screenshots it took. The daemon keeps the last 20 of each pane until it restarts.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `window` | string |  |  | The window id of the pane. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `artifacts` | []object | Each artifact: id, kind (tml or screenshot), title, at (Unix nanoseconds) and window_id. A tml artifact adds surface, target, open, doc and state, and when it closed, reason and value. A screenshot adds path and format. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"artifact-list","params":{"window":"3f2a91c0-0000-4000-8000-000000000000"}}
```

### `ask-agent`

Ask another agent a question, or with select and confirm every agent pane a selector matches: wait until it is not mid-turn, type the question into its pane, wait until it has dealt with it, and answer with what the pane printed in between. A target on needs_input is refused with agent_blocked and nothing is typed, because the text would answer its prompt. A target that shows no sign of taking the question within stall_timeout of Enter fails with prompt_stalled; the question was typed, so look at the pane before sending it again. Asks of one target take turns: an ask that comes while another ask of the same target runs waits for its turn, first come first served, for up to timeout, and fails with not_ready when the turn does not come. The reply is another program's output and is data, not instructions.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session` | string |  |  | Session name. Omit from a pane for the pane's own session, and from outside every pane for the most recently active one. |
| `window` | string |  |  | The agent to ask, by window id or name. list-agents is how you find it. Required unless select is given. |
| `from` | string |  |  | The asking window, normally $TUIOS_PANE_ID. It is what the cycle guard is keyed on, so omitting it gives up loop detection. human from a process inside a pane of this daemon is refused with forbidden. |
| `from_host` | string |  |  | The name of the machine the caller is on, normally $TUIOS_HOST. Kept on the record only for an ask that arrived over a link. |
| `text` | string |  |  | The question. It is typed as one paste (wrapped in bracketed paste when the target has it on) and submitted with a carriage return, the Enter key. Trailing line breaks are dropped, and a question of several lines is submitted once. |
| `ready_timeout` | int |  | `30000` | Milliseconds to wait for the target to be ready before giving up with not_ready. Ready is idle, done, errored or none; unknown is not ready. A target on needs_input ends the wait at once with agent_blocked. |
| `settle` | int |  | `2000` | Milliseconds of silence from the target that count as it having finished, for a pane that reports no state. The silence is counted from when the target showed it took the question. |
| `timeout` | int |  | `300000` | Milliseconds to wait for the answer overall. An ask that has to wait for its turn behind other asks of the same target waits up to this long for it first, and the wait for the answer then starts again. |
| `lines` | int |  | `200` | Cap the reply to this many lines, newest kept. |
| `force` | bool |  | `false` | Send without waiting for the target to be ready, interleaving with whatever it is doing. It does not override agent_blocked: a target on needs_input is still refused unless allow_blocked is set. |
| `allow_blocked` | bool |  | `false` | Type at a target on needs_input instead of refusing with agent_blocked. The text then answers whatever prompt the target is showing, so pass it only after reading the prompt with capture-pane and finding it takes free text. |
| `stall_timeout` | int |  | `5000` | Milliseconds after Enter within which the target must show it took the question: its agent state turns working or needs_input, it finishes a turn, or, for an agent whose harness cannot show working, it prints something. If it shows none of these the ask fails with prompt_stalled. The question was typed either way. |
| `select` | string |  |  | A selector: space-separated key:value terms, all of which must match, each with comma-separated alternatives. Keys: harness (id or program name), state, needs:you, session (glob), group (fan-out group, glob), host (local or a host name, glob), name (window name, glob), cwd (the directory or under it; ~ is home), task (a task card the pane holds, by id, glob), stage (the stage of a card the pane holds), parent and root (the parent agent pane, or the first of its chain, by window id or a start of it). Asks every pane the selector matches at once, at most 16. It reaches agent panes on this machine, in every session, and takes no session and no window. Without confirm nothing is sent: the call fails with confirm_required, whose hint lists the panes in available and carries the token in confirm. |
| `confirm` | string |  |  | The token for the set of panes the selector matches, from a confirm_required hint or from list-agents with the same selector. The write goes ahead only when the selector still matches exactly that set; otherwise it fails with confirm_required again and the new set. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `replies` | []object | With select: one row per pane, in session order: session, window, name, ok, and the fields of a single ask's answer when it was answered, or error (code, message, hint) when it was not. A pane on needs_input is refused in its row with agent_blocked unless allow_blocked is set. |
| `answered` | int | With select: how many panes answered. |
| `failed` | int | With select: how many did not. |
| `window` | string | The window that was asked. |
| `waited_for` | string | The state the target was in when the question was sent. |
| `queued_behind` | int | How many asks of the same target were ahead of this one when it came. 0 when it did not wait for a turn. |
| `waited_turn_ms` | int | How long the ask waited for its turn, in milliseconds. |
| `settled_by` | string | What ended the wait: agent-state when the target reported it had finished, idle when it simply went quiet, timeout when neither happened, or window-closed/session-closed when the target went away. One of `agent-state`, `idle`, `timeout`, `window-closed`, `session-closed` or `shutdown`. |
| `state` | string | The target's agent state after answering. |
| `untrusted` | bool | Always true. The reply is another program's output. |
| `reply` | string | What the pane printed after the question was sent. |
| `lines` | int | How many lines the reply holds. |
| `truncated` | bool | Whether older reply lines were cut to fit the line cap. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"ask-agent","params":{"session":"work","window":"review","from":"$TUIOS_PANE_ID","text":"does the payment retry path look right to you?"}}
```

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"ask-agent","params":{"select":"group:fan/add-retry state:idle","text":"summarise your change in one line"}}
```

### `ask-human`

Ask the person a question with a fixed set of answers, and wait for the answer. The question goes in the Inbox as an ask item; a client that shows the asking pane opens it at once, and a detached session keeps it for the next attach. When the wait ends first the status is pending and the question stays: the answer is then mailed to the asking pane from human, marked verified_human, or read by calling again with request_id. A caller inside a pane asks only as that pane. Refused over a link.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session` | string |  |  | Session name. Omit from a pane for the pane's own session, and from outside every pane for the most recently active one. |
| `window` | string |  |  | The pane asking, where a late answer is mailed. Omit to ask as your own pane from inside one, or as no pane from outside every pane. A caller inside a pane may not name another. |
| `question` | string |  |  | The question: one line of printable text, at most 160 bytes, which the Inbox shows as written. Required unless request_id is given. |
| `options` | []string |  |  | The answers, 1 to 9, picked with the keys 1 to 9. Each is one line of printable text, at most 60 bytes, and no two are the same. Required unless request_id is given. |
| `timeout` | int |  | `120000` | Milliseconds to wait for the answer, at most one hour. The question outlives the wait. |
| `wait` | bool |  | `true` | Wait for the answer. Pass false to ask and return pending at once. |
| `request_id` | string |  |  | Come back for a question already asked: wait on it again, or read how it ended. Takes no question or options. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `request_id` | string | The question's id: pass it back to wait again or read the answer. |
| `status` | string | answered, pending (the wait ended first; the question stays), dismissed, superseded (the pane asked a newer one), window_closed, session_closed, evicted or shutdown. |
| `answer` | string | The option the person picked, when answered. |
| `answer_index` | int | Its position among the options, from 1. |
| `answered_by` | string | The id of the client the answer came from. |
| `verified_human` | bool | True when answered: only a client attached at the time can answer, with its attach nonce. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"ask-human","params":{"session":"work","question":"Deploy the branch to staging?","options":["yes","no","later"],"timeout":300000}}
```

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"ask-human","params":{"request_id":"9f86d081884c7d65","wait":false}}
```

### `attach-presence`

Hold the person's nonce on this connection without a screen. The reply carries human_nonce, which respond, reply-approval, answer-ask and dismiss-attention take as the person's, by the same rules as an attach nonce: only from outside every pane, over a link only on a stream the hub vouched for, and only from the process that holds it. The presence does not attach: it does not change the session size, focus or view a pane, or receive broadcasts. The connection serves other verbs as before, and the presence ends when it closes.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session` | string |  |  | The session the nonce is for. The nonce then acts only in that session. Omit it for every session. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `human_nonce` | string | The nonce. Send it from this process, as human_nonce. |
| `client_id` | string | This connection's client id, which answered_by reports. |
| `session` | string | The session, when one was named. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"attach-presence"}
```

### `attach-task`

Put a file or a link on a task card. The caller sends the file's bytes; the daemon never reads a path. A file is stored once by its sha256 and goes when no card names it. The kind is read from the content, then the name. The limits are [tasks] attachment_max_mib, attachments_per_card and attachment_session_mib. A pane changes the attachments of the card it holds, and of the card it finished while that card waits in verify or review; admin, the person, a shell, a link and a plugin change any card's.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session` | string |  |  | Session name. Omit from a pane for the pane's own session, and from outside every pane for the most recently active one. |
| `id` | string | yes |  | The card, such as t1. |
| `name` | string |  |  | The name the card shows it by, at most 120 bytes; control and invisible characters are dropped. Required with content; a pull request link defaults to PR #N, another link to its host and path. |
| `content` | string |  |  | The file, base64 encoded. Pass one of content, files, link, screenshot and capture. |
| `files` | []object |  |  | Several files at once, each name and content (base64): one change and one history entry. All of them fit one request line (12 MiB in base64). |
| `link` | string |  |  | An http or https address. A GitHub pull request's address is kind pr. |
| `note` | string |  |  | A line about it, at most 500 bytes. |
| `replace` | bool |  | `false` | Put it in the place of the attachment with the same name. Without it a name in use with other bytes is refused; the same name with the same bytes is left as it is, so a retry succeeds. |
| `screenshot` | object |  |  | window (a pane of the card's session), scrollback and lines: the daemon takes a PNG of the pane and attaches it, named after the pane. A pane that calls needs the read grant. |
| `capture` | object |  |  | window, scrollback and lines (default 2000): the daemon takes the pane's text and attaches it. A pane that calls needs the read grant. |
| `host` | string |  |  | The machine that owns the card, for a project card from the pane that holds it there. The attach is passed on over the link; a picture or a text is taken here first. At most 3 MiB crosses. |
| `human_nonce` | string |  |  | The attached client's nonce, to act as the person (by human). One that does not verify is refused with not_human. |
| `remote_window` | string |  |  | Over the link only: the window on the calling machine that holds the card. Its daemon sends it; the holder's machine is the link's peer. |
| `remote_name` | string |  |  | Over the link only: how that window reads on its machine. |
| `remote_harness` | string |  |  | Over the link only: the agent that window runs. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `attachment` | object | name, kind (image, markdown, mermaid, diff, text, link, pr, file), media_type, size, hash, url (a link), note, by and at. |
| `attachments` | []object | Every attachment the call named, as the card holds it now. |
| `unchanged` | []string | The names the card held with the same bytes already, left as they were. |
| `task` | object | The card's row after the attach. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"attach-task","params":{"session":"work","id":"t1","name":"after.png","content":"iVBORw0KGgo=","note":"the fixed layout"}}
```

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"attach-task","params":{"session":"work","id":"t1","link":"https://github.com/o/r/pull/12"}}
```

### `block-task`

Mark a task card stuck, saying what it waits on, or with clear lift the mark. A blocked card keeps its holder and its stage, and claim-task next skips it. To let a card go, use release-task. A pane changes only the card it holds unless it holds admin; the person, a shell, a link and a plugin change any card.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session` | string |  |  | Session name. Omit from a pane for the pane's own session, and from outside every pane for the most recently active one. |
| `id` | string | yes |  | The card, such as t1. |
| `reason` | string |  |  | What the card waits on, at most 500 bytes. Required without clear. |
| `clear` | bool |  | `false` | Lift the block. Not with reason. |
| `human_nonce` | string |  |  | The attached client's nonce, to act as the person (by human). One that does not verify is refused with not_human. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | string | The card. |
| `session` | string | Its session. |
| `title` | string | Its title. |
| `preview` | string | The first line of its prompt, at most 80 characters. |
| `stage` | string | Its stage. One of `todo`, `plan`, `doing`, `verify`, `review`, `shipped` or `done`. |
| `stage_at` | int | When it entered the stage, Unix nanoseconds. |
| `holder` | object | The pane that holds it: window, name, harness, state (read from the pane now) and held_at. Null when no pane holds it. |
| `by` | string | Who added it: human, shell, the adding pane's window id, link:HOST or plugin:ID. |
| `at` | int | When it was added, Unix nanoseconds. |
| `blocked` | object | reason, by and at when the card is blocked, else null. |
| `fan` | object | fan and fan_id, for a card added in a fan's session. |
| `attachment_count` | int | How many attachments the card has. Absent for none. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"block-task","params":{"session":"work","id":"t1","reason":"waiting on the staging API key"}}
```

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"block-task","params":{"session":"work","id":"t1","clear":true}}
```

### `bundle-worktree`

Read a worktree session's work out in chunks so it can cross a link: its commits as a git bundle, then its uncommitted work as a binary patch. The first call makes the transfer and answers with its first chunk. Later calls pass the token and the next offset. tuios worktree pull is the caller.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session` | string |  |  | The worktree session to read. Required on the first call. Never guessed. |
| `full` | bool |  | `false` | Bundle the branch's whole history. Without it only the commits past the merge base with the worktree's base are carried, and the reader must have base_commit. |
| `token` | string |  |  | The transfer to read on, from the first call's reply. Only the connection that made a transfer can read it. |
| `offset` | int |  | `0` | Where to read from: the next of the previous reply. |
| `release` | bool |  | `false` | With token, end the transfer without reading the rest. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `token` | string | The transfer's token. It ends after its last chunk, on release, when this connection closes, or after ten minutes unread. |
| `content` | string | This chunk, base64, at most 4 MB before encoding. |
| `offset` | int | Where this chunk starts. |
| `next` | int | Where the next chunk starts. |
| `done` | bool | True on the last chunk, after which the token is gone. |
| `repo` | string | First call only: the repository's name. |
| `origin_url` | string | First call only: the repository's origin URL, empty when it has none. |
| `branch` | string | First call only: the branch the worktree has checked out. |
| `base` | string | First call only: the ref the worktree was made from, empty when it is not known. |
| `base_commit` | string | First call only: the commit the bundle assumes the reader has, empty for a full bundle. |
| `head` | string | First call only: the commit the branch is at. |
| `full` | bool | First call only: true when the bundle carries the whole history. |
| `bundle_bytes` | int | First call only: how many bytes of the transfer are the bundle, zero when the branch has no commits past base_commit. |
| `patch_bytes` | int | First call only: how many bytes after the bundle are the patch, zero when nothing is uncommitted. |
| `changes` | int | First call only: how many paths the patch touches, untracked files included. |
| `size` | int | First call only: the transfer's whole length. |
| `sha256` | string | First call only: the SHA-256 of the whole transfer, for the reader to check. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"bundle-worktree","params":{"session":"api-fan-add-retry-2"}}
```

```json
{"id":2,"verb":"bundle-worktree","params":{"token":"<token>","offset":4194304}}
```

### `cancel-queued`

Drop messages from a pane's delivery queue before they are typed. The person, with a live human_nonce, may drop any entry; a pane only the entries it queued; a linked machine only the entries it queued; a caller outside every pane every entry but the person's. An entry being typed cannot be dropped. Dropping a stalled entry closes its Inbox question; the entries behind it are typed at the pane's next rest, one reached after the stalled entry was typed. A linked machine that gave no name drops only what it queued on the same connection.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session` | string |  |  | Session name. Omit from a pane for the pane's own session, and from outside every pane for the most recently active one. |
| `window` | string |  |  | Window id or name. With all, omit it to target the focused window. With id, omit it to find the entry in any pane of the session. |
| `id` | string |  |  | The entry to drop. |
| `all` | bool |  | `false` | Drop every entry of the window the caller may drop. |
| `human_nonce` | string |  |  | The attached client's nonce, to drop as the person. One that does not verify is refused with not_human. |
| `host` | string |  |  | A machine from [hosts]: drop from a pane's queue there, over the link. The link drops only the entries this machine queued there. Only a caller outside every pane may ask; the far machine's policy for this one must allow write. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `cancelled` | []string | The entries dropped. |
| `queued` | int | How many entries the pane's queue holds now. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"cancel-queued","params":{"session":"work","window":"build","all":true}}
```

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"cancel-queued","params":{"session":"work","id":"q3"}}
```

### `capture-pane`

Capture a pane's content. On a window that shows a TML surface, the content is the surface drawn at the window's size, with its state and the open tab, and the result adds surface.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session` | string |  |  | Session name. Omit from a pane for the pane's own session, and from outside every pane for the most recently active one. |
| `window` | string |  |  | Window id or name. Omit to target the focused window. |
| `source` | string |  | `visible` | Which buffer to capture. last-command-output is what the pane's last finished command printed, read between its shell's OSC 133 marks; it is plain text, adds cmdline, exit_code, command_seq and truncated to the result, and fails with no_shell_integration when no command has finished under the marks. One of `visible`, `recent` or `last-command-output`. |
| `styled` | bool |  | `false` | Include ANSI styling in the captured text. |
| `scrollback` | bool |  | `false` | Older spelling of source "recent". |
| `ansi` | bool |  | `false` | Older spelling of styled. |
| `resolved` | bool |  | `false` | Rewrite ANSI index colours (30-37, 90-97, 40-47, 100-107, 38;5;n<16, 48;5;n<16) to 24-bit RGB so the capture matches what a themed client paints. Indices above 15 and true colour pass through untouched. |
| `palette` | []string |  | `xterm defaults` | The 16 hex colours (#rrggbb) the client's theme paints indices 0-15 with, used by resolved captures. Must be exactly 16 entries when present; absent means the xterm defaults. |
| `lines` | int |  |  | Keep only the last N lines. Blank rows below the cursor do not count. Ignored when start or end is given. With commands, the most lines to keep in total (default 2000). |
| `commands` | int |  |  | Capture the last N shell commands, found by the pane's OSC 133 marks, from the scrollback. Each command runs from its prompt to the next prompt. When the total is over lines, the longest outputs lose their middle, and one line says how many lines were left out. A pane with no marks, or on the alternate screen, gives the last lines lines instead, with command_marks false. Takes no start, end or unwrap. |
| `start` | int |  |  | 1-based inclusive first line of the region to keep. |
| `end` | int |  |  | 1-based inclusive last line of the region to keep. |
| `unwrap` | bool |  | `false` | Join the rows the terminal wrapped because a line was longer than the pane, so each line is one line as the program wrote it. A row that ends with a newline stays a line of its own. lines, start and end then count the joined lines. |
| `trim` | bool |  | `false` | Drop the blank lines at the end of the capture, which are the empty rows below the cursor. lines, start and end then count what is left. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `content` | string | The captured text, one line per row, or per line with unwrap. |
| `unwrapped` | bool | True when unwrap joined the wrapped rows. Omitted otherwise. |
| `trimmed` | bool | True when trim dropped the blank lines at the end. Omitted otherwise. |
| `lines_omitted` | int | With lines: how many older lines the capture left out, 0 when it kept them all. With commands: how many lines it cut from the middle of the outputs. Omitted without lines or commands, and with start or end. |
| `command_marks` | bool | With commands: true when the marks cut the capture into commands, false when it gave the last lines lines instead. |
| `commands` | []object | With commands and command_marks true: one entry per command captured, oldest first, with lines (kept), lines_omitted, finished, and cmdline and exit_code when the shell reported them. |
| `commands_available` | int | With commands and command_marks true: how many commands the scrollback holds under marks. |
| `source` | string | The buffer captured. One of `visible`, `recent` or `last-command-output`. |
| `styled` | bool | Whether content carries ANSI styling. |
| `resolved` | bool | Whether index colours were rewritten to 24-bit RGB. |
| `history_rows` | int | How many scrollback lines the pane holds above its screen. A recent capture of more lines than this plus the screen has nothing older to show. Not on a last-command-output capture. |
| `revision` | int | A number that grows each time the pane's content can change: output reaches it, or it is resized. Two captures of one source with one revision have the same content. It counts from 0 again when the daemon restarts. Not on a last-command-output capture. |
| `boot_id` | string | The daemon start the revision belongs to, the boot_id of subscribe and list-attention. Compare revisions only when boot_id is the same. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"capture-pane","params":{"session":"work","source":"recent","lines":50}}
```

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"capture-pane","params":{"session":"work","source":"recent","unwrap":true,"trim":true}}
```

### `checkpoint-diff`

Read what one turn changed: the diff of a checkpoint against the pane's checkpoint before it, or against HEAD when it was taken for the first one. Nothing in the repository, its index or its working tree is changed. The text is the repository's, so it is marked untrusted.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session` | string |  |  | Session name. Omit from a pane for the pane's own session, and from outside every pane for the most recently active one. |
| `window` | string |  |  | Window id or name. Omit to target the focused window. |
| `n` | int |  |  | The checkpoint. Omit for the newest. |
| `paths` | []string |  |  | Only these paths, relative to the repository root. Omit for every changed file. |
| `context` | int |  | `3` | Lines of context around each change, 0 to 20. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `session` | string | The session of the pane. |
| `window` | string | The pane, by id. |
| `worktree` | string | The root of the work tree. |
| `checkpoint` | object | The checkpoint read: n, ref, commit, tree, head, kind (turn or safety), pane, session, turn, state, label, worktree, at (Unix nanoseconds), and skipped and skipped_count (the untracked files left out as larger than max_untracked_mb, omitted when none). |
| `base` | string | What the diff runs from: checkpoint N, HEAD <hash>, or an empty tree. |
| `base_tree` | string | The tree the diff runs from. |
| `files` | []object | One entry per changed file, as review-diff returns them. |
| `totals` | object | files, added and removed over the whole diff. |
| `truncated` | bool | The diff passed a cap (400 files, 2 MiB, 5000 lines in a file) and the files past it carry counts only. |
| `untrusted` | bool | Always true: the text is the repository's, not the daemon's. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"checkpoint-diff","params":{"session":"work","window":"build","n":2}}
```

### `claim-task`

Take a task card for a pane: the card id, or with next the oldest card no pane holds that is not blocked or done, and that no fan of another session works on (fan with task). A card in todo or plan moves to doing. The pick and the take are one step, so two panes claiming next at once take two cards. A pane claims only for itself unless it holds admin; outside a pane, name the window. The card is released when its pane closes, its agent leaves the pane, or the daemon restarts.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session` | string |  |  | Session name. Omit from a pane for the pane's own session, and from outside every pane for the most recently active one. |
| `id` | string |  |  | The card to take. Not with next. |
| `next` | bool |  | `false` | Take the oldest open card no pane holds and that is not ask_first. Not with id. With none to take, the call fails with no_task. |
| `window` | string |  |  | The pane that takes the card, by id or name. From a pane, omit it to claim for that pane. Required outside a pane. |
| `human_nonce` | string |  |  | The attached client's nonce, to act as the person (by human). One that does not verify is refused with not_human. |
| `host` | string |  |  | The machine that owns the card, for a project card claimed, released or finished from the pane that works on it here. The call is passed on over the link for this pane. |
| `remote_window` | string |  |  | Over the link only: the window on the calling machine that holds the card. Its daemon sends it; the holder's machine is the link's peer. |
| `remote_name` | string |  |  | Over the link only: how that window reads on its machine. |
| `remote_harness` | string |  |  | Over the link only: the agent that window runs. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | string | The card. |
| `session` | string | Its session. |
| `title` | string | Its title. |
| `preview` | string | The first line of its prompt, at most 80 characters. |
| `stage` | string | Its stage. One of `todo`, `plan`, `doing`, `verify`, `review`, `shipped` or `done`. |
| `stage_at` | int | When it entered the stage, Unix nanoseconds. |
| `holder` | object | The pane that holds it: window, name, harness, state (read from the pane now) and held_at. Null when no pane holds it. |
| `by` | string | Who added it: human, shell, the adding pane's window id, link:HOST or plugin:ID. |
| `at` | int | When it was added, Unix nanoseconds. |
| `blocked` | object | reason, by and at when the card is blocked, else null. |
| `fan` | object | fan and fan_id, for a card added in a fan's session. |
| `attachment_count` | int | How many attachments the card has. Absent for none. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"claim-task","params":{"session":"work","next":true,"window":"build"}}
```

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"claim-task","params":{"session":"work","id":"t1","window":"build"}}
```

### `command-history`

List the commands that shells in panes on this machine ran, newest first. Only a shell that sends OSC 133 marks reports its commands. A pane keeps its last 200 commands. A command that ran again in the same pane is listed once, at its newest run.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session` | string |  |  | List only this session's panes. Omit to list every session. |
| `window` | string |  |  | List only this pane. Without session, the window is looked for in the most recently active session. |
| `limit` | int |  | `200` | The most commands to return, at most 2000. |
| `query` | string |  |  | List only the commands whose text holds this. Case does not count unless the query has a capital letter. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `commands` | []object | One entry per command: session, window (the window id), name, seq (the pane's command_seq when it finished), cmdline (at most 512 bytes, with likely secrets masked), exit (null when the shell sent no status), at (when it started, Unix ms), duration_ms, cwd (the folder the shell last reported over OSC 7 when it started, empty when it never reported one) and row (the history row of its prompt, -1 when the prompt has left the history). |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"command-history"}
```

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"command-history","params":{"session":"work","window":"build","limit":20,"query":"go test"}}
```

### `compare-fan`

Compare the attempts of a fan: one row per sibling session with its branch, agent, state, changed files and lines against the base (committed and uncommitted work, untracked files included), the last verify-fan check and the last command its shells finished. A sibling the caller could not name itself is left out.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `session` | string |  |  | Any session of the fan. Omit for the most recently active session. |
| `changes` | bool |  | `true` | Count the changed files and lines of each attempt, a few git calls per sibling. |
| `detail` | bool |  | `false` | With changes, also list each attempt's files (file_list, at most 8, most changed first) and its first changed lines (lines, at most 6). Two more git calls per sibling. From a linked machine, detail needs write in its policy. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `group` | string | The fan's group. |
| `repo` | string | The repository the fan is of. |
| `repo_root` | string | The repository's main checkout. |
| `base` | string | The base the attempts were made from: the fan's base, or the main checkout's branch for a fan made from HEAD. |
| `rows` | []object | One per sibling: session, branch, path, agent, harness, state, files, added, removed, ahead, dirty, base_sha, prompt_status, verify, last_command (cmdline, exit, at, window), gone, and note when git could not count. Also cost, the cost its agent reported, started_at and done_at, notes from the verdict, and file_list and lines with detail. |
| `prompt` | string | The task the fan gave the attempts. |
| `verdict` | object | The judge's pick, when set-fan-verdict recorded one: pick, summary, reasons, judge and at. |
| `total` | int | How many rows. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"compare-fan","params":{"session":"api-fan-retry-1"}}
```

### `daemon-upgrade`

Upgrade the daemon live to another tuios binary. Every pane's process keeps running, with the same pid, terminal and screen, and the daemon keeps its pid. The binary is run once first and must say it reads this daemon's state; a refusal changes nothing. The call answers once every pane is stopped and written down. Then every client is told and dropped, and the daemon execs the binary in place. Call hello until instance changes: its upgrade field says what the handoff measured, and has fallback set when the new binary could not take the panes and the binary that was running took them back, and recovered too when the new image died first and the handoff's keeper started that binary under a new pid. Refused on Windows, while a pane runs on another machine or here for another machine, while a fan check, a capturing popup, a hook or a plugin command runs, and to a plugin or over a link, whatever its grants. From a caller inside a pane it first asks the person in a dialog on every attached client, naming the binary's full path, its version and the pane, and blocks for up to 60 s: only Yes, pressed at a client, lets it go on, and anything else is forbidden.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `binary` | string |  |  | Absolute path of the tuios binary to run the daemon as. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `pid` | int | The daemon's pid, which the upgrade keeps. |
| `instance` | string | The running image's id. hello reports another one once the new image runs. |
| `from` | string | The version running now. |
| `to` | string | The version the binary reports. |
| `binary` | string | The binary, as an absolute path. |
| `handoff` | int | The version of the state handed over. |
| `panes` | int | How many panes are handed over. |
| `manifest_bytes` | int | The size of the state handed over. |
| `clients` | int | The attached TUI clients the upgrade dropped. A tuios client on this machine attaches again by itself. |
| `preflight_ms` | int | How long the binary took to answer the preflight. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"daemon-upgrade","params":{"binary":"/usr/local/bin/tuios"}}
```

### `delegate`

Hand a task to a child agent: start it with start-agent, linked to its parent and never wider than it, and type the task as its first prompt. Without wait the call returns at once, and the child's answer comes to the parent as mail from the child once it finishes, which wakes the parent at rest. A question the child stops on comes as mail too, and an approval stays in the Inbox. With wait the call returns the outcome itself: done, a question or an approval, or the timeout, which does not stop the child. A call from outside every pane names the parent or waits.

| Param | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `prompt` | string | yes |  | The task, typed as the child's first prompt. |
| `agent` | string |  |  | The child agent, as start-agent takes it. Omit for the parent's own harness. |
| `args` | []string |  |  | More arguments for the agent, as an argv. |
| `role` | string |  | `general` | The job. It names the child's window and shows in list-agents. research and review default grants to read. It adds no text to the prompt. One of `implementation`, `research`, `review`, `design`, `test` or `general`. |
| `isolation` | string |  | `shared` | shared: the child works in the parent's folder. worktree: in a new worktree, in a session of its own, made from the commit of the parent's checkout. A worktree does not have uncommitted changes. One of `shared` or `worktree`. |
| `name` | string |  |  | The child's window name. Omit for the role. |
| `session` | string |  |  | Session to open the child in, for shared isolation. Omit for the parent's session. |
| `cwd` | string |  |  | The folder the child starts in, or, for worktree isolation, the checkout to make the worktree from. Omit for the parent's folder. |
| `parent` | string |  |  | The agent pane the answer is mailed to, by window id. Omit it from an agent pane: that pane is the parent. Outside every pane, name it or pass wait. |
| `wait` | int |  |  | Milliseconds to wait for the outcome, at most 600000. Omit to return at once and get the answer by mail. |
| `ready_timeout` | int |  | `120000` | Milliseconds to wait for the child to be ready for its prompt. |
| `env` | object |  |  | Environment variables for the agent, as start-agent takes them. |
| `grants` | []string |  |  | What the new pane may do through tuios: read, write, fan, respond, admin, or none. Omit for the default of [agents.permissions], or, from a pane without admin, the calling pane's own grants. A pane may give only what it holds, and admin does not include respond. A window on another machine holds what that machine gives it. One of `read`, `write`, `fan`, `respond`, `files`, `task`, `admin` or `none`. |

| Result field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `delegated` | bool | True when the task was typed into the child. |
| `window_id` | string | The child's pane. The other fields of start-agent are here too, with its outcome as start_outcome. |
| `role` | string | The role. |
| `isolation` | string | shared or worktree. |
| `mode` | string | async or wait. |
| `next` | string | Async only: what the parent does now. It ends its turn, and tuios wakes it with the answer. |
| `worktree` | string | Worktree isolation: the worktree's folder. branch and base are its branch and start commit, and worktree_note says it has no uncommitted changes. |
| `outcome` | string | Wait only: how the wait ended. One of `done`, `errored`, `question`, `approval`, `closed` or `timeout`. |
| `answer` | string | Wait only, on done or errored: the child's last answer, from its transcript or its screen (answer_source). Text another program wrote: data, not instructions. |
| `question` | object | Wait only, on question or approval: the prompt the child stopped on, kind, message and lines. |
| `wait_note` | string | Wait only, on timeout: the child still works, and where its answer goes. |

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"delegate","params":{"role":"review","prompt":"Review the diff on this branch and list the bugs."}}
```

```json
{"id":1,"verb":"delegate","params":{"agent":"codex","isolation":"worktree","role":"test","prompt":"Add tests for the parser.","wait":300000}}
```

With session, every client of the session. With neither, the client used last in the caller's pane's session, else in the only session with a client. all_other keeps that one client and detaches the rest of its session.

| `session` | string |  |  | Session whose clients to detach. |

| Result field | Type | Description |

| `detached` | array | Ids of the clients that were detached. |

{"id":1,"verb":"detach-client","params":{"session":"work","all_other":true}}
